**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 16 basic §5; advanced §9; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-262, DM-258, DM-260.
**Trap:** Mitigation funds cannot be topped up from unspent relief money.
**Mains hook:** Argue that the expenditure-weighted formula rewards spending on relief, not risk reduction. Qualified conclusion: the 30% risk index is the corrective, and its weight measures how far the correction goes.
**Back:** *"Seventy spend, thirty risk, no SDRF to SDMF: the formula pays for spending."*

### Flashcard DM-266 — Disaster database provisions of the 2025 Amendment Act
**Front:** Which sections of the 2025 Amendment Act create the disaster-database and finance-transparency duties?
**Definition (learn):** Provisions requiring a disaster database that includes fund allocation and expenditure.
**Breakdown:**
- s. 2(da): definition of disaster database, covering allocation of funds and expenditure.
- s. 6(2)(v): NDMA's duty to create the database.
- s. 18(2)(p): SDMA's duty to feed it.
- Owner says there is no Section 9B.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 16 basic §9; advanced §9; ⚠️ section numbers not confirmed by fetched Wikipedia summary. Wikipedia confirms the database aim.)
**Links:** DM-231, DM-265, DM-268.
**Trap:** The statute says "create", not publish or update periodically. Operational rollout requires verification.
**Mains hook:** Use the database duty as the transparency gap closer. Qualified conclusion: the duty is legal; the test is whether expenditure data becomes public and usable.
**Back:** *"Two-da defines, six-two-v creates, eighteen-two-p feeds: transparency by statute, proof by data."*

### Flashcard DM-267 — Financing layers by loss frequency
**Front:** Which financing instrument matches which loss frequency?
**Definition (learn):** A layered financing model in which each instrument covers a band of loss.
**Breakdown:**
- Routine, high-frequency loss: budget reserve and local contingency.
- Medium loss: disaster funds and contingent credit.
- Severe, low-frequency loss: insurance, reinsurance, catastrophe instruments and sovereign support.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ REVISION-CHART §20; owner 16 basic §13 link.)
**Links:** DM-255, DM-256, DM-268.
**Trap:** Insurance is for severe, low-frequency losses; using it for routine loss is a mismatch.
**Mains hook:** Argue that layering protects fiscal stability. Qualified conclusion: the layers must be sized by the loss distribution, not by political appetite.
**Back:** *"Reserve the routine, fund the medium, insure the severe: match the layer to the loss."*

### Flashcard DM-268 — Relief finance, mitigation finance, risk transfer and risk reduction
**Front:** How do relief finance, mitigation finance, risk transfer and risk reduction differ?
**Definition (learn):** Four pairs that must not be treated as synonyms.
**Breakdown:**
- Relief finance: post-event assistance (SDRF/NDRF).
- Mitigation finance: ex-ante risk reduction (SDMF/NDMF).
- Risk transfer: shifts a defined financial loss contractually (insurance, catastrophe bonds).
- Risk reduction: lowers hazard, exposure or vulnerability.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ REVISION-CHART §20, §23; owner 16 basic §13.)
**Links:** DM-255, DM-257, DM-258.
**Trap:** A relief disbursement is neither a mitigation outcome nor a recovery. Insurance does not physically reduce risk.
**Mains hook:** Classify each instrument by its purpose. Qualified conclusion: compensation after an event is not the same as lowering risk before it.
**Back:** *"Relief pays after, mitigation prevents before, transfer shifts the bill, reduction shrinks the hazard."*

### Flashcard DM-269 — Build Back Better: Sendai Priority 4 and the BBB test
**Front:** What is Build Back Better, and what test does the owner propose?
**Definition (learn):** Reconstruction that reduces, not recreates, pre-existing vulnerability; Sendai Priority 4.
**Breakdown:**
- Sendai Priority 4: enhance preparedness for effective response and Build Back Better in recovery, rehabilitation and reconstruction.
- Test: safer location, stronger standard, restored livelihood, inclusive services, ecological safeguards, maintenance finance.
- Applies to management systems as well as structures (CDRI, owner).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 16 basic §2, §13; PDF p.62; REVISION-CHART §20.)
**Links:** DM-238, DM-241, DM-270.
**Trap:** Rebuilding the old state is not BBB. Insurance alone does not deliver BBB.
**Mains hook:** Test reconstruction against the six-part BBB test. Qualified conclusion: BBB is judged by safer location, service continuity and livelihoods, not by the amount paid.
**Back:** *"Location, standard, livelihood, inclusion, ecology, maintenance: six tests, not one cheque."*

### Flashcard DM-270 — Advanced: ex-ante and ex-post finance asymmetry (proportion, procedure, transferability)
**Front:** What three-part asymmetry does the advanced file identify in disaster finance?
**Definition (learn):** The mitigation window is structurally weaker than the response window on three counts.
**Breakdown:**
- Proportion: mitigation capped at 20% of the State corpus against 80% response.
- Procedure: mitigation releases are project-by-project on High Level Committee approval under s. 47; SDRF is formula-driven.
- Transferability: FC16 forbids SDRF-to-SDMF transfer.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ analysis of owner 16 advanced §1-2.)
**Links:** DM-258, DM-265, DM-267.
**Trap:** Do not say India has no mitigation fund; say it is capped and approval-dependent.
**Mains hook:** Argue that India priced mitigation at one-fifth of response. Qualified conclusion: closing the investment gap requires reweighting and routinising the ex-ante window, not only creating it.
**Back:** *"Twenty against eighty, approved one by one, never transferred: the mitigation door is narrow."*

### Flashcard DM-271 — Advanced: relief adequacy versus mitigation creation as designed ceiling
**Front:** How does the advanced file describe the trade-off between relief adequacy and mitigation?
**Definition (learn):** A budgetary trade-off resolved by formula, which caps ex-ante investment by design.
**Breakdown:**
- FC15 and FC16 both fix 80:20 response to mitigation, so mitigation grows only with relief.
- FC16 shifted preparedness financing into the smaller mitigation window without enlarging its share.
- The owner calls this a designed ceiling, not an accident.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ analysis of owner 16 advanced §7.)
**Links:** DM-270, DM-261, DM-262.
**Trap:** Do not confuse relief compensation with full recovery financing; SDRF finances immediate relief, a narrower category.
**Mains hook:** Argue that the formula makes ex-ante investment a residual. Qualified conclusion: a formula that ties mitigation to relief growth cannot achieve a resilience transition on its own.
**Back:** *"Mitigation grows only as relief grows: a ceiling written into the formula."*

### GROUP — 17 Humanitarian Logistics, Relief, Rehabilitation and Recovery (basic + advanced)

### Flashcard DM-272 — Three post-disaster phases: relief, rehabilitation, reconstruction
**Front:** Define the three post-disaster phases and their objectives.
**Definition (learn):** Sequential phases of post-disaster response, each with a distinct objective.
**Breakdown:**
- Relief: search and rescue, rapid damage and needs assessment, first aid, temporary shelter, humanitarian assistance (immediate survival).
- Rehabilitation: enable basic services to resume, revive economic activity, psychosocial support; transitional, may be temporary.
- Reconstruction: full restoration of services and infrastructure, integrated into long-term development with future risk accounted for.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 17 basic §1-2; PDF pp.8-9.)
**Links:** DM-273, DM-276, DM-288, DM-269.
**Trap:** Relief distribution is not recovery. Each phase is distinct, and conflating them is the common error.
**Mains hook:** Name the phase precisely in any response answer. Qualified conclusion: fast relief is necessary but says nothing about rehabilitation or risk-informed reconstruction.
**Back:** *"Survive, function, rebuild safer: three phases, one order, no shortcuts."*

### Flashcard DM-273 — Incident Command System (2003) versus India's Incident Response System (2010)
**Front:** How does the generic ICS differ from India's Incident Response System?
**Definition (learn):** ICS is the generic management model (2003) organising functions and staff during incidents; India's arrangement is the Incident Response System, governed by NDMA's July 2010 guidelines.
**Breakdown:**
- ICS: generic model; deactivated once rehabilitation ends; normal administration then handles remaining reconstruction (owner-stated).
- IRS: India's institutional arrangement; NDMA Guidelines on Incident Response System, July 2010.
- Use "IRS" for India; reserve "ICS" for the generic model.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 17 basic §2, §8; PDF p.9. IRS guidelines date 📰 owner-stated.)
**Links:** DM-272, DM-274, DM-280.
**Trap:** ICS and IRS are not interchangeable labels in an Indian answer. ICS does not continue through reconstruction.
**Mains hook:** Name the Indian structure and the handover point. Qualified conclusion: the handover to normal administration is an accountability transition; its timing needs explicit criteria.
**Back:** *"ICS is the model from 2003, IRS is India's 2010 rules: say IRS when you mean India."*

### Flashcard DM-274 — NDRF: composition, pre-positioning and battalions
**Front:** What is the NDRF's composition, its pre-positioning practice, and its battalion count?
**Definition (learn):** The specialist disaster response force under NDMA's general superintendence and the DG, NDRF.
**Breakdown:**
- Specialist search-and-rescue composition: engineers, technicians, paramedics and dog squads.
- Proactive availability and pre-positioning ahead of threatening events, credited with minimising damage (owner).
- 16 battalions (confirmed by fetched Wikipedia NDRF page; owner: MHA, 22 July 2025).
- Sanctioned strength 18,581 is owner-stated and not fetched.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 17 basic §2, §5; PDF p.13; ✅ Wikipedia "National Disaster Response Force" (16 battalions); 18,581 ⚠️.)
**Links:** DM-275, DM-276, DM-277, DM-287.
**Trap:** NDRF is not a generic security or law-and-order force. Do not state the sanctioned strength without a dated source.
**Mains hook:** Describe NDRF as a specialist layer with anticipatory practice. Qualified conclusion: pre-positioning is effective where lead time exists; for sudden hazards, readiness and local capacity matter more.
**Back:** *"Engineers, technicians, paramedics, dogs: sixteen battalions, positioned before the storm."*

### Flashcard DM-275 — NDRF: constitution and statutory basis
**Front:** When and under which provision of the DM Act was the NDRF constituted?
**Definition (learn):** A specialist force constituted under the Disaster Management Act, 2005, which provides for the National Disaster Response Force.
**Breakdown:**
- Wikipedia infobox formation date: 19 January 2006 (conflicting text says 2005; treat the date as ⚠️).
- Statutory basis: ss. 44-45 of the DM Act 2005 (Wikipedia Act summary: "Sections 44-45 provide for the National Disaster Response Force").
- Head: Director General, an IPS officer (Wikipedia).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ Wikipedia "Disaster Management Act, 2005" (ss. 44-45); ⚠️ NDRF formation date conflicting on Wikipedia.)
**Links:** DM-274, DM-256 (fund, not force).
**Trap:** The NDRF is a force (ss. 44-45); the National Disaster Response Fund is a fund (s. 46). Do not confuse them.
**Mains hook:** Cite the statutory basis when asserting NDRF's legal role. Qualified conclusion: the force is statutory, but its operational effectiveness depends on State coordination.
**Back:** *"Forty-four to forty-five builds the force; forty-six funds the response."*

### Flashcard DM-276 — State Disaster Response Force (s. 44A, 2025)
**Front:** What does the owner say s. 44A provides for States?
**Definition (learn):** Owner-stated provision enabling States to constitute their own specialist State Disaster Response Force.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: s. 44A inserted in 2025 and enables States to raise their own SDRF (State Disaster Response Force).
- Counterpart to the national NDRF.
- Existence requires a State constitution and operating capacity.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ owner 17 basic §5; not confirmed by fetched Wikipedia summary of the Act.)
**Links:** DM-274, DM-275, DM-257.
**Trap:** The State Disaster Response Force (force) is not the State Disaster Response Fund (SDRF, s. 48). Enabling power is not an existing force.
**Mains hook:** Argue that enabling power must be followed by notified, trained State forces. Qualified conclusion: s. 44A closes the State-tier gap only where a State constitutes and equips the force.
**Back:** *"Forty-four A enables, the State must constitute: a force on paper is not a force on the ground."*

### Flashcard DM-277 — Aapda Mitra: community volunteer scheme
**Front:** What is the Aapda Mitra scheme and what is its expanded target?
**Definition (learn):** A scheme training community volunteers for relief and rescue when emergency services are not readily available.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: trains volunteers for flood, flash-flood and urban-flood relief and rescue, before a flood in flood-prone districts.
- Expanded target: 1,00,000 volunteers across 350 districts (MHA, 25 July 2024, owner-stated).
- Earlier coverage (2016 pilot: 30 districts, 25 States) is document-period.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 17 basic §5, §8; PDF p.29; 2024 target 📰 owner-stated, not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-274, DM-278, DM-287.
**Trap:** The 30-district pilot is not current coverage. Do not present volunteers as substitutes for trained State responders.
**Mains hook:** Position Aapda Mitra as the community layer that complements NDRF. Qualified conclusion: volunteers need training, equipment and institutional support to be an asset, not a shift of State responsibility.
**Back:** *"Aapda Mitra: a million helpers across three hundred fifty districts, trained before the flood."*

### Flashcard DM-278 — Yuva Aapda Mitra: youth volunteer programme
**Front:** What is Yuva Aapda Mitra?
**Definition (learn):** A separate youth-focused programme under the Aapda Mitra umbrella, cross-referenced to topic 03.
**Breakdown:**
- Youth-volunteer programme distinct from the adult Aapda Mitra scheme (owner-stated).
- Developed in topic 03 (community-based DRR).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §5; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-277, DM-287.
**Trap:** Do not conflate the youth programme's coverage with the adult scheme's coverage.
**Mains hook:** Use youth volunteering as capacity building for the next generation of responders. Qualified conclusion: youth programmes build capacity only where training and supervision are sustained.
**Back:** *"Young helpers, same training: the youth scheme builds the next responder, not the current one."*

### Flashcard DM-279 — Minimum Standards of Relief (February 2016)
**Front:** Which NDMA guideline sets relief-camp benchmarks, and when was it issued?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA's guideline setting minimum standards for relief provision, issued under the DM Act.
**Breakdown:**
- Guidelines on Minimum Standards of Relief, February 2016.
- Sets the benchmark for relief-camp provision.
- Cite the standard, not administrative discretion.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §2, §5; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-272, DM-280, DM-281.
**Trap:** Minimum standards are not maximum or current adoption. Guidelines are advisory and their district adoption needs evidence.
**Mains hook:** Measure relief against the named standard. Qualified conclusion: standards define the floor; dignity and inclusion require more than the floor.
**Back:** *"Minimum in February 2016: the floor for the camp, not the ceiling for the dignity."*

### Flashcard DM-280 — Temporary Shelters for Disaster-Affected Families (September 2019)
**Front:** Which NDMA guideline covers temporary shelters and when was it issued?
**Definition (learn):** The NDMA guideline for the shelter stage of relief.
**Breakdown:**
- Guidelines on Temporary Shelters for Disaster-Affected Families, September 2019.
- Covers the shelter stage of relief.
- Owner names it as one of the two documents to cite.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §2, §5; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-279, DM-272, DM-282.
**Trap:** Do not use a generic "shelter guidelines" reference; name the September 2019 document.
**Mains hook:** Argue that temporary shelter must include protection, accessibility and dignity. Qualified conclusion: the guideline is a standard, and implementation is measured at the shelter, not on paper.
**Back:** *"September 2019 for the shelter: temporary, not temporary-forever."*

### Flashcard DM-281 — Management of the Dead and Comprehensive Disaster Victim Identification
**Front:** What guidance governs management of the dead and victim identification?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA guidance on dignified handling of the dead and identification of disaster victims.
**Breakdown:**
- Management of the Dead in the Aftermath of Disaster: August 2010.
- Comprehensive Disaster Victim Identification and Management guidelines: January 2026 (owner-stated).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §5, advanced §10; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-279, DM-280, DM-282.
**Trap:** Owner-listed dates are not fetched; verify before quoting January 2026 as an issuance date.
**Mains hook:** Argue that identification and dignity are relief functions, not afterthoughts. Qualified conclusion: the 2026 guideline expands relief toward dignity, but district adoption is unreported.
**Back:** *"Ten for the dead, twenty-six for the identity: dignity is part of relief."*

### Flashcard DM-282 — Mental Health and Psychosocial Support Services in Disasters (December 2023)
**Front:** What is the NDMA MHPSS guideline and when was it updated?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA guidance on mental health and psychosocial support in disasters, updated in December 2023 (owner-stated).
**Breakdown:**
- Covers psychosocial support during relief and rehabilitation.
- Part of the 2024-2026 guideline cluster that expands relief toward service continuity and dignity.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §5, advanced §10; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-272, DM-281, DM-283.
**Trap:** Psychosocial support is a relief and rehabilitation function, not optional.
**Mains hook:** Include mental health as a rehabilitation outcome. Qualified conclusion: guidelines make psychosocial support standard, but its reach depends on trained staff.
**Back:** *"Mind after the flood: psychosocial support belongs in relief, not in the aftermath."*

### Flashcard DM-283 — Emergency Operations Centre guidance (October 2024)
**Front:** What guidance covers EOCs, and when was it issued?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA guidance on establishing and operating Emergency Operations Centres.
**Breakdown:**
- Emergency Operations Centre establishment and operations: October 2024 (owner-stated).
- Standing EOC design is now guideline-backed rather than improvised (owner's advanced reading).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §5, advanced §10; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-273, DM-285, DM-274.
**Trap:** A guideline is not proof that every district has a functioning EOC.
**Mains hook:** Use EOCs as the operational centre of coordination. Qualified conclusion: an EOC is only as good as its staffing, drills and linkage to the district.
**Back:** *"Operations centre, October 2024: the room where command becomes coordination."*

### Flashcard DM-284 — International Humanitarian Assistance and Disaster Relief (HADR) guidance (October 2024)
**Front:** What does the HADR guidance cover, and what is its limit?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA guidance on international humanitarian assistance and disaster relief, October 2024 (owner-stated).
**Breakdown:**
- Governs how foreign assistance enters and is coordinated.
- International assistance supplements national responsibility.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §5; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-303, DM-310, DM-285.
**Trap:** International aid must fit domestic command and accountability; it cannot bypass local command.
**Mains hook:** Argue that international assistance must integrate with national command. Qualified conclusion: HADR guidance clarifies the route, not the local outcome.
**Back:** *"Foreign help, national command: HADR guidance says the front door is domestic."*

### Flashcard DM-285 — Disaster Management Exercises guidance (October 2024)
**Front:** What does the October 2024 exercises guidance add to preparedness?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA guidance on rehearsing response through drills and exercises.
**Breakdown:**
- Disaster Management Exercises: October 2024 (owner-stated).
- Drills test coordination, communication and equipment before an event.
- Owner: rehearsed exercises are now guideline-backed.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §5, advanced §10; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-283, DM-284, DM-272.
**Trap:** Drills are not outcome evidence; measure whether the drill changed response.
**Mains hook:** Use drills as a preparedness measure with an evaluation test. Qualified conclusion: exercises matter when they are repeated and their lessons are fed back.
**Back:** *"Rehearse to reveal the gap: the drill is the test, not the trophy."*

### Flashcard DM-286 — Tsunami response: layered logistics
**Front:** What logistics combination does the tsunami example illustrate?
**Definition (learn):** Specialist equipment and community-based actors operating together in relief.
**Breakdown:**
- Inflatable motorised boats, helicopters and search-and-rescue equipment "required immediately after a tsunami" (PDF p.24).
- Deployed alongside SHGs, NGOs and CBOs.
- Illustrates simultaneous, not sequential, layering.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 17 basic §6, advanced §4; PDF p.24.)
**Links:** DM-287, DM-274, DM-277.
**Trap:** The example illustrates logistics layers; it is not proof of response speed or outcomes.
**Mains hook:** Use layered logistics to show that specialist and community capacity must coexist. Qualified conclusion: the layering works when both actors are trained and coordinated.
**Back:** *"Boats from above, neighbours beside: the tsunami needed both at once."*

### Flashcard DM-287 — Anticipatory versus reactive humanitarian logistics
**Front:** Why is anticipatory logistics more effective where warning exists?
**Definition (learn):** Pre-positioning relief and responders ahead of a forecast event reduces loss compared with purely reactive mobilisation.
**Breakdown:**
- Anticipatory: NDRF pre-positioning; pre-stocked relief; pre-identified shelters.
- Reactive: used where no anticipatory action was possible (earthquake, unforeseen event).
- Applicability depends on lead time: cyclones and floods (hours to days) versus earthquakes (no prediction) and cloudbursts (very short).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 17 basic §3, advanced §1-2; ⚠️ analysis.)
**Links:** DM-274, DM-277, DM-272, DM-291.
**Trap:** Pre-positioning cannot guarantee reach. Earthquakes and cloudbursts need readiness and stockpiling, not forecast-based action.
**Mains hook:** Design logistics by hazard type and lead time. Qualified conclusion: anticipatory logistics is the most effective form where a forecast exists; everywhere else it reduces to readiness.
**Back:** *"Forecast, pre-position, then strike the impact: where the sky warns, logistics moves first."*

### Flashcard DM-288 — Advanced: two-speed restoration strategy
**Front:** What does "temporary, then full" restoration mean in the owner's analysis?
**Definition (learn):** Rehabilitation restores lifelines temporarily and faster; full standard restoration is deferred to reconstruction.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: "even on a temporary basis" for roads, bridges, airports, ports and helipads (PDF p.9).
- Accepts a lower standard for speed during rehabilitation.
- Full restoration is the reconstruction phase's task.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ analysis of owner 17 advanced §7; PDF p.9.)
**Links:** DM-272, DM-289, DM-290.
**Trap:** Do not present temporary restoration as final restoration; the handover must be explicit.
**Mains hook:** Explain the two-speed strategy and its risk of becoming permanent. Qualified conclusion: temporary restoration is necessary, but its exit date must be tracked.
**Back:** *"Temporary first, full later: the bridge that stays temporary is the risk to watch."*

### Flashcard DM-289 — Advanced: rapid assessment versus equitable accuracy
**Front:** What trade-off does the advanced file name between assessment speed and accuracy?
**Definition (learn):** Rapid damage and needs assessment enables prompt relief, but without disaggregation it can miss vulnerable groups.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: "rapid damage and needs assessments" precede provision of relief (PDF p.9).
- Risk: assessment error misallocates relief; correction comes later, in rehabilitation.
- Disaggregation by disability, gender, age and displacement status is needed to avoid under-identifying needs.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ analysis of owner 17 advanced §6-7.)
**Links:** DM-272, DM-281, DM-288.
**Trap:** Procedural rapidity does not mean equitable accuracy.
**Mains hook:** Argue for disaggregated rapid assessment. Qualified conclusion: speed and equity are compatible only if assessments record who is being missed.
**Back:** *"Fast needs, slow mistakes: disaggregate the count or lose the excluded."*

### Flashcard DM-290 — Advanced: reconstruction financing gap
**Front:** Which financing gap does the advanced file flag for reconstruction?
**Definition (learn):** SDRF and NDRF name relief and rehabilitation expenditure explicitly, but not a dedicated reconstruction instrument.
**Breakdown:**
- SDRF finances immediate relief; broader recovery needs supplementary support.
- Reconstruction is where BBB's risk-reducing investment is most needed.
- Owner calls this a potential gap, not a confirmed one.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ analysis of owner 17 advanced §9; owner 16 advanced §7.)
**Links:** DM-269, DM-262, DM-270.
**Trap:** Do not claim a reconstruction fund exists unless a dated source says so.
**Mains hook:** Name the reconstruction-financing gap and link it to BBB. Qualified conclusion: without a dedicated reconstruction window, BBB depends on ad hoc budgeting.
**Back:** *"Relief is funded, rebuilding is hoped for: name the reconstruction gap before it bites."*

### Flashcard DM-291 — NDMA SACHET alert system
**Front:** What is SACHET, and why is it cited as a current anchor?
**Definition (learn):** NDMA's official alert and warning system, named by the owner as a current anchor for relief-coordination technology.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: SACHET and Aapda Mitra official systems checked as of 18 July 2026.
- Used to verify current relief-coordination technology.
- Owner warns that VisionIAS's document-period descriptions must not be presented as current scope.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 17 basic §9; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-287, DM-301, DM-306.
**Trap:** An alert system's reach is not receipt, comprehension or action; test all three.
**Mains hook:** Use SACHET as a warning-reach example and test its last-mile outcome. Qualified conclusion: a system is an input; reach and action are the outcomes.
**Back:** *"Sachet sends the message; the last mile decides the meaning."*

### GROUP — 18 Governance, Capacity and International Cooperation (basic + advanced)

### Flashcard DM-292 — Yokohama Strategy for a Safer World (1994)
**Front:** What does the Yokohama Strategy (1994) set out?
**Definition (learn):** Ten principles from the mid-term review of the International Decade for Natural Disaster Reduction.
**Breakdown:**
- Ten principles: risk assessment; development-integrated prevention and preparedness; capacity development; early warning; participatory prevention; vulnerability reduction through education; technology-sharing; environmental protection; each country's primary protection responsibility with international support.
- Hosted in Yokohama, Japan (first of three WCDRR conferences in Japan).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §1-2; PDF p.60.)
**Links:** DM-293, DM-294, DM-297.
**Trap:** Yokohama is a voluntary strategy, not a treaty.
**Mains hook:** Trace the Yokohama-Hyogo-Sendai lineage. Qualified conclusion: the strategies accumulate lessons; their binding force never increased.
**Back:** *"Ten principles in ninety-four: the first draft of the modern DRR vocabulary."*

### Flashcard DM-293 — Hyogo Framework for Action (2005-2015)
**Front:** What are the five priorities of the Hyogo Framework for Action?
**Definition (learn):** The first plan to detail the work required from all sectors and actors to reduce disaster losses, adopted at Kobe in 2005.
**Breakdown:**
- Make DRR a priority.
- Improve risk information and early warning.
- Build a culture of safety and resilience.
- Reduce underlying risk in key sectors.
- Strengthen preparedness for response.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §2; PDF p.61.)
**Links:** DM-292, DM-294, DM-298.
**Trap:** Hyogo has five priorities; Sendai has four. Do not invert the counts.
**Mains hook:** Use Hyogo's five priorities as the baseline for the Sendai comparison. Qualified conclusion: Hyogo named the work; Sendai added measurable targets.
**Back:** *"Five priorities, two thousand five to fifteen: Hyogo names the work, Sendai counts it."*

### Flashcard DM-294 — Sendai Framework (2015-2030): four priorities
**Front:** What are the four Sendai priorities?
**Definition (learn):** The four priorities of the Sendai Framework for DRR, adopted at Sendai, Japan, March 2015, covering 2015-2030.
**Breakdown:**
- Understanding disaster risk.
- Strengthening disaster-risk governance.
- Investing in DRR for resilience.
- Enhancing preparedness for effective response, and Build Back Better in recovery, rehabilitation and reconstruction.
- Succeeds the Hyogo Framework (2005-2015).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ Wikipedia "Sendai Framework for Disaster Risk Reduction", fetched; ✅ owner 18 basic §2.)
**Links:** DM-293, DM-295, DM-269.
**Trap:** Priority 4 includes BBB; do not state BBB as a separate priority.
**Mains hook:** Map each priority to a national measure. Qualified conclusion: the four priorities are a checklist; the outcome test is the targets.
**Back:** *"Understand, govern, invest, prepare and rebuild: four priorities, one Sendai."*

### Flashcard DM-295 — Sendai seven global targets and Target E
**Front:** What are the seven Sendai global targets, and which has the 2020 deadline?
**Definition (learn):** Seven quantified global targets of Sendai, lettered A-G in common usage (the fetched Wikipedia source numbers them 1-7).
**Breakdown:**
- A: reduce mortality.
- B: reduce the number of affected people.
- C: reduce direct economic loss relative to GDP.
- D: reduce damage to critical infrastructure and disruption of basic services.
- E: increase national and local DRR strategies (2020 deadline; the only one).
- F: enhance international cooperation with developing countries.
- G: expand access to multi-hazard early warning and risk information.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ Wikipedia (fetched: seven targets, Target 5 = national and local strategies, 2020 deadline); lettering A-G is owner/UNDRR convention ⚠️.)
**Links:** DM-294, DM-300, DM-301.
**Trap:** Target E carries the 2020 deadline; the rest are 2030. Targets A-D measure losses, E-G are means of implementation.
**Mains hook:** Use the target groups in a Sendai comparison. Qualified conclusion: the seven targets make progress measurable, but measurement needs reporting completeness.
**Back:** *"A to D count losses, E to G count the means: E alone had its deadline in twenty-twenty."*

### Flashcard DM-296 — Legal status of Yokohama, Hyogo and Sendai
**Front:** Are the Yokohama, Hyogo and Sendai frameworks binding treaties?
**Definition (learn):** They are voluntary, non-binding frameworks adopted at UN World Conferences.
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: all three are voluntary or non-binding.
- Fetched Wikipedia page: stakeholders called for "a legally-based instrument" during Sendai negotiations; it does not say the call was met.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §8; ✅ Wikipedia Sendai page (legal status not confirmed as binding).)
**Links:** DM-292, DM-293, DM-294.
**Trap:** Do not call Sendai a binding treaty. A target does not establish national implementation.
**Mains hook:** Qualify the frameworks' force. Qualified conclusion: non-binding frameworks work through peer review and reporting, not enforcement.
**Back:** *"Voluntary frameworks, unbound treaties: the commitment is political, the pressure is reporting."*

### Flashcard DM-297 — Concept: cumulative evolution of the DRR frameworks
**Front:** How did the three frameworks build on one another?
**Definition (learn):** Each framework built on and formalised its predecessor's lessons.
**Breakdown:**
- Yokohama (1994): ten principles.
- Hyogo (2005-2015): five priorities.
- Sendai (2015-2030): four priorities and seven targets.
- Shift: from listing priorities to quantified targets and means of implementation.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §1, §3.)
**Links:** DM-292, DM-293, DM-294, DM-298.
**Trap:** Do not conflate the frameworks by date or count.
**Mains hook:** Argue that evolution moved from description to measurement. Qualified conclusion: measurement without local implementation remains incomplete.
**Back:** *"Ten principles, five priorities, four priorities and seven targets: the ladder that counts."*

### Flashcard DM-298 — 2018 UPSC PYQ: Hyogo versus Sendai comparison
**Front:** How should the 2018 Mains question on DRR before and after Sendai be answered?
**Definition (learn):** The 2018 question asked for measures before and after Sendai and a comparison with Hyogo (250 words, 15 marks).
**Breakdown:**
- Measures before and after signing Sendai (2015-2030).
- Difference between Sendai and Hyogo Framework for Action (2005).
- Structure: period, five versus four priorities, seven targets, shift to measurable means.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §6; PDF p.62; 2018 outside the audited 2024-2025 window.)
**Links:** DM-293, DM-294, DM-297.
**Trap:** Do not describe Sendai in isolation; the question requires comparison.
**Mains hook:** Use the before-after comparison as the template. Qualified conclusion: Sendai's targets raise the bar, but domestic implementation determines the result.
**Back:** *"Before and after, Hyogo and Sendai: compare first, describe second."*

### Flashcard DM-299 — Sendai Midterm Review (2023) and UNGA resolution 77/289
**Front:** When was the Sendai Midterm Review, and what did it produce?
**Definition (learn):** The high-level meeting that reviewed Sendai's first half and produced a political declaration.
**Breakdown:**
- High-Level Meeting: 17-19 May 2023.
- Political Declaration issued as UN General Assembly resolution 77/289 (18 May 2023, owner-stated).
- A midterm political milestone, not the latest data snapshot.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §2, §7; ✅ owner 15 advanced §10; not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-300, DM-295.
**Trap:** Do not cite the midterm review as the current data. Use the 2026 UNDRR monitor for current status.
**Mains hook:** Use the midterm review as a political checkpoint. Qualified conclusion: a declaration renews commitment, but outcomes need monitoring data.
**Back:** *"Seventeen to nineteen May twenty-twenty-three: a declaration at midterm, not a scorecard."*

### Flashcard DM-300 — UNDRR Sendai monitor status (24 September 2026)
**Front:** What do the 2026 UNDRR Sendai monitoring figures report?
**Definition (learn):** Official UNDRR global progress figures from the Sendai Framework Monitor.
**Breakdown:**
- 176 countries reporting (fetched; as of April 2026).
- Average disaster mortality per 100,000: 2.12 (2005-2014) to 0.75 (2016-2025), down 65%.
- Affected-person rate per 100,000: 1,227 to 2,449, roughly doubled.
- Reported direct economic losses 2016-2025: above US$1 trillion.
- 151 countries with national DRR strategies (up from 56 in 2015).
- 128 countries reporting MHEWS (up from 55 in 2015).
- Release dated 24 September 2026 in the text (page header shows 25 September).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ UNDRR news release, fetched; release date ambiguous by one day.)
**Links:** DM-295, DM-299, DM-301.
**Trap:** These are reported global trends, not causal proof, uniform national performance or India-specific achievement. Reporting completeness varies.
**Mains hook:** Read the global trend and the affected-people rise together: mortality fell, but exposure grew. Qualified conclusion: fewer deaths with more people affected means exposure and reporting need separate scrutiny.
**Back:** *"One hundred seventy-six countries, mortality down sixty-five percent, affected doubled: fewer deaths, more people in harm's way."*

### Flashcard DM-301 — Early Warnings for All (EW4All)
**Front:** What is Early Warnings for All, and what is its target date?
**Definition (learn):** A UN initiative launched by the Secretary-General in March 2022 and at COP27 (November 2022), aiming for universal early-warning coverage.
**Breakdown:**
- Target: universal early-warning coverage by 2027 (owner-stated).
- Pillars: risk knowledge (UNDRR), observation and forecasting (WMO), dissemination (ITU), preparedness and response (IFRC).
- Operational vehicle for Sendai Target G.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner 18 basic §2, §9; ⚠️ not fetched.)
**Links:** DM-295, DM-300, DM-291.
**Trap:** A warning system's coverage is not its effectiveness; test receipt and action.
**Mains hook:** Use EW4All as the dated international early-warning target. Qualified conclusion: the target is measurable, but last-mile reach is the test.
**Back:** *"Universal warning by twenty-seven: four pillars, one last mile."*

### Flashcard DM-302 — National Platform for DRR (NPDRR): composition and advisory role
**Front:** What is NPDRR, who chairs it, and what is its function?
**Definition (learn):** A multi-stakeholder national platform reviewing and advising on DRR policy implementation.
**Breakdown:**
- Chaired by the Union Home Minister.
- Vice-Chairpersons: MoS (DM, MHA) and NDMA Vice-Chairman.
- Members: State and UT DM ministers, mayors of six named metros, four Lok Sabha and two Rajya Sabha members, ten ULB chairpersons.
- Function: review progress from time to time; appraise implementation; advise on Centre-State coordination (PDF p.12).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §2, §3; PDF p.12.)
**Links:** DM-303, DM-308, DM-292.
**Trap:** NPDRR advises and reviews; it does not direct or bind States.
**Mains hook:** Describe NPDRR's advisory role and its accountability gap. Qualified conclusion: a broad platform without binding power depends on political pressure.
**Back:** *"Chair the Home Minister, review and advise: the platform that cannot direct."*

### Flashcard DM-303 — Global Platform for DRR (GPDRR)
**Front:** What is the GPDRR, and what is its role?
**Definition (learn):** The international forum dedicated to DRR, recognised by the UN General Assembly as a contribution to the High-Level Political Forum on Sustainable Development.
**Breakdown:**
- Convened since 2007.
- Latest in the owner's list: Bali, Indonesia, 2022, co-chaired by Indonesia and UNDRR.
- Function: review progress on Sendai implementation, feed the HLPF.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §2, §5; PDF pp.63-64.)
**Links:** DM-302, DM-304, DM-295.
**Trap:** GPDRR is a review and knowledge-exchange forum, not an enforcement body.
**Mains hook:** Name the forum's review function and its non-binding nature. Qualified conclusion: review forums build accountability through reporting, not sanctions.
**Back:** *"Global forum, review not rule: GPDRR feeds the HLPF and holds the mirror."*

### Flashcard DM-304 — India's hosted international DM events (2015-2023)
**Front:** Which international DM events did India host, with years and places?
**Definition (learn):** Owner's chronology of India-hosted international DM events as evidence of convening leadership.
**Breakdown:**
- SAADMex 2015.
- BRICS DM Ministers' Meeting 2016 (Udaipur).
- AMCDRR 2016 (New Delhi).
- BIMSTEC DMEx-2017; SAARC DM Centre inauguration at GIDM 2017.
- IWDRI 2018; India-Japan DRR Workshop 2018.
- SCOJtEX 2019.
- G20 DRR Working Group 2023.
- 6th World Congress on Disaster Management 2023 (Dehradun).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §6; PDF pp.63-65.)
**Links:** DM-303, DM-305, DM-307.
**Trap:** Hosting shows convening, not local resilience. Do not present the 2015-2023 chronology as current cooperation intensity without verification.
**Mains hook:** Use the dense chronology as evidence of diplomatic leadership, then test local outcomes. Qualified conclusion: convening power is real, but it is a different category from local DRR performance.
**Back:** *"Eight hosts, one decade: India convenes; the last mile still has to be proven."*

### Flashcard DM-305 — Media's dual role in disasters
**Front:** What positive and negative roles does the owner give media?
**Definition (learn):** Media influences government priorities and information, but can also exaggerate or panic the public.
**Breakdown:**
- Positive: pressure on government prioritisation; exposes preparedness-expenditure inefficiency; continuous factual coverage that assists authorities and volunteers.
- Negative: exaggeration, panic-inducing coverage, sensationalised misreporting.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §4; PDF p.66.)
**Links:** DM-306, DM-309, DM-291.
**Trap:** Do not present media as uniformly beneficial.
**Mains hook:** Balance media's dual role with a verification protocol. Qualified conclusion: accredited, factual briefing reduces the panic risk without silencing the press.
**Back:** *"Media informs or inflames: the same camera does both."*

### Flashcard DM-306 — Social media in disaster communication: Hudhud example and limits
**Front:** What does the owner say social media can and cannot do in disasters?
**Definition (learn):** Social media enables one-to-one, one-to-many and many-to-many communication that often remains active when conventional channels fail.
**Breakdown:**
- Capabilities: device-independent, real-time or asynchronous communication.
- Hudhud cyclone (Visakhapatnam) WhatsApp-group coordination as a concrete case (PDF p.66).
- Limits: cannot and should not supersede current approaches or replace existing infrastructure (PDF p.67).
- Risks: technology failure, hacking, rumour spread.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §4; PDF pp.66-67.)
**Links:** DM-305, DM-291, DM-309.
**Trap:** Social media cannot fully replace official or conventional communication.
**Mains hook:** Argue for formal integration of informal digital tools. Qualified conclusion: social media extends reach and resilience of communication, but cannot be the sole channel.
**Back:** *"Hudhud's WhatsApp: useful when the towers fail, never the whole warning system."*

### Flashcard DM-307 — Advanced: localisation gap between national visibility and district implementation
**Front:** What critique does the advanced file make of India's cooperation record?
**Definition (learn):** India's diplomatic and hosting leadership is extensive, but the translation of frameworks into district and community practice is less documented.
**Breakdown:**
- National-level visibility and diplomatic standing: strong.
- District and community localisation: the weakest, least source-detailed link.
- NPDRR's review function is not described at the district level in the source.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ analysis of owner 18 advanced §1-2, §6.)
**Links:** DM-302, DM-304, DM-310.
**Trap:** International hosting is not domestic localisation. Do not conflate diplomatic achievement with domestic outcomes.
**Mains hook:** Separate diplomatic leadership from district outcome. Qualified conclusion: India's DRR leadership is credible internationally only when district warning and relief are measured as strongly.
**Back:** *"Global stage, local stairs: the cooperation climbs, the district must follow."*

### Flashcard DM-308 — Advanced: NPDRR accountability-design gap
**Front:** What accountability gap does the advanced file identify in NPDRR's design?
**Definition (learn):** Review "from time to time" is weaker than binding, time-bound reporting, and no published dashboard or escalation consequence is described.
**Breakdown:**
- Review and appraisal function (PDF p.12).
- No standardised monitoring dashboard or defined escalation in the source.
- Advanced answer should name this as a design gap, not a finding of failure.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ analysis of owner 18 advanced §9.)
**Links:** DM-302, DM-307.
**Trap:** Do not claim NPDRR has enforcement power; claim the design lacks it.
**Mains hook:** Propose time-bound reporting and a published dashboard. Qualified conclusion: review without consequences produces reports, not implementation.
**Back:** *"From time to time is not a timetable: review needs a dashboard and a consequence."*

### Flashcard DM-309 — Advanced: media coordination protocol gap
**Front:** What institutional gap does the advanced file flag for media?
**Definition (learn):** Media's dual role is named but not backed by a formal coordination protocol.
**Breakdown:**
- No accredited-briefing system during active disasters is described in the source.
- Owner suggests formal coordination to reduce panic and misreporting.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (⚠️ analysis of owner 18 advanced §8.)
**Links:** DM-305, DM-306.
**Trap:** Do not present a briefing protocol as an existing national requirement without a source.
**Mains hook:** Propose an accredited-briefing protocol. Qualified conclusion: a protocol reduces rumour without restricting factual reporting.
**Back:** *"Accredit the briefing, verify the rumour: the protocol is the missing source."*

### Flashcard DM-310 — International partner network for DRR operations
**Front:** Which international bodies form the UNDRR/UNISDR partnership network the owner lists?
**Definition (learn):** The operational partners in disaster response, information and finance cooperation.
**Breakdown:**
- UNOCHA (humanitarian coordination).
- UNDAC (UN Disaster Assessment and Coordination).
- INSARAG (international urban search and rescue).
- GFDRR (Global Facility for Disaster Reduction and Recovery).
- SDMC-IU, ADRC (Asian Disaster Reduction Centre), ADPC (Asian Disaster Preparedness Center), ARF.
- Owner also names bilateral and regional instruments, e.g. SAARC Rapid Response Agreement.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 18 basic §3, §5; PDF p.63.)
**Links:** DM-303, DM-284, DM-307.
**Trap:** Partner networks supply surge and information, but do not replace national command.
**Mains hook:** Map each partner to its function. Qualified conclusion: cooperation adds surge, data and finance, and must fit domestic command.
**Back:** *"OCHA coordinates, UNDAC assesses, INSARAG searches, GFDRR funds: each partner has one job."*

## DISASTER MANAGEMENT — Cyclones, Floods, Drought, Landslides, Forest Fire, Industrial/CBRN

Scope: owners 07–12 (basic + advanced), REVISION-CHART_Hazards-Risk-and-Distinctive-Features.md. Tags: `(✅ owner 07-basic)` = repository owner text; `(✅ Wikipedia: <page>)` = fetched 30 Oct 2026; `⚠️` = owner-only, unverified, conflicting or memory-only. Owner figures marked "document-period" are not current without a dated source.

### GROUP — Cyclones, Storm Surge and Coastal Preparedness

### Flashcard DM-311 — Six requirements for tropical cyclogenesis
**Front:** What conditions are needed for a tropical cyclone to form?
**Definition (learn):** Tropical cyclogenesis needs six simultaneous conditions over a warm ocean.
**Breakdown:**
- Sea-surface temperature sufficiently warm.
- Atmospheric instability.
- High humidity in lower-to-middle troposphere.
- Sufficient Coriolis force (so the system can rotate; near-zero at the equator).
- A pre-existing low-level disturbance.
- Low vertical wind shear.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §2, PDF pp.35-36).
**Links:** DM-312 (storm surge), DM-327 (warning chain), DM-313 (eye structure).
**Trap:** A warm sea alone does not produce a cyclone; the six conditions must coincide. Strong vertical wind shear suppresses a developing system.
**Mains hook:** GS-III coastal-risk answers: explain why the Bay of Bengal, not every warm sea, generates the storms that the warning chain must track. Conclusion: cyclone risk is predictable in climatology but location-specific in timing, so preparedness must be seasonal and standing.
**Back:** *"SIHCDW — Sea hot, Instability, Humidity, Coriolis, Disturbance, Wind-calm."*

### Flashcard DM-312 — Storm surge
**Front:** Define storm surge and state what drives it.
**Definition (learn):** Storm surge is an abnormal rise of sea level driven mainly by storm winds and low atmospheric pressure, distinct from the astronomical tide.
**Breakdown:**
- Driven by wind stress pushing water onshore and by low pressure raising sea level.
- Severity depends also on coast shape, bathymetry and tidal phase (owner caveat: no universal surge height).
- It is a compound flood: it combines with rain and river flow.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §13.1; REVISION-CHART §11 "storm surge").
**Links:** DM-311, DM-326 (Odisha 1999), DM-319 (NDMA cyclone guidelines), DM-320 (NCRMP saline embankments).
**Trap:** Surge is not the same as cyclone wind damage; a cyclone can be "weak" on the wind scale yet deadly through surge. Do not quote a universal surge metre figure.
**Mains hook:** Use surge to show cyclone risk is compound (wind + rain + surge + river/urban flood). Conclude that saline embankments and bio-shields address surge, while shelters address people; neither addresses rain-driven flood alone.
**Back:** *"Surge is the sea's wind-push, not the moon's tide."*

### Flashcard DM-313 — Cyclone eye, eyewall and the location of peak winds
**Front:** Where are cyclone winds strongest, and what is the eye?
**Definition (learn):** The eye is the calm, low-pressure centre (very little cloud or rain); the eyewall surrounds it and carries the strongest winds.
**Breakdown:**
- Winds peak roughly 20–30 km from the calm eye, then decline (owner: "often exceeding 150 km/h").
- Eyewall = strongest winds and convection (REVISION-CHART §11).
- Air rises in the surrounding cloud bands; the eye is where air descends.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §1-2, PDF p.35; ✅ REVISION-CHART §11).
**Links:** DM-311, DM-314 (wind classification).
**Trap:** "Winds are strongest at the eye" is the classic wrong option. The eye is calm; damage concentrates in the eyewall band.
**Mains hook:** Explain why evacuation-zone design must account for the eyewall band rather than the storm centre; a qualified close is that track uncertainty widens the zone.
**Back:** *"Calm eye, angry wall, peak 20–30 km out."*

### Flashcard DM-314 — IMD cyclone wind-speed classification
**Front:** State the IMD wind-speed categories from Depression to Super Cyclonic Storm.
**Definition (learn):** IMD classifies cyclonic disturbances by 3-minute sustained wind speed in knots (kt) and km/h.
**Breakdown:** (owner values; Wikipedia table in brackets where it differs)
- Depression 17–27 kt (31–49 km/h) [Wikipedia: 31–50 km/h].
- Deep Depression 28–33 kt (50–61 km/h) [Wikipedia: 51–62 km/h].
- Cyclonic Storm 34–47 kt (62–88 km/h) [Wikipedia: 63–88 km/h].
- Severe Cyclonic Storm 48–63 kt (89–117 km/h).
- Very Severe Cyclonic Storm 64–89 kt (118–166 km/h) [Wikipedia: 118–165 km/h].
- Extremely Severe Cyclonic Storm 90–119 kt (167–221 km/h) [Wikipedia: 166–220 km/h].
- Super Cyclonic Storm ≥120 kt (≥222 km/h) [Wikipedia: ≥221 km/h].
- A system is named only from the Cyclonic Storm stage.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §2 citing IMD/RSMC New Delhi 2022 edition — ⚠️ not fetched; ✅ Wikipedia: Tropical_cyclone_scales — table differs at km/h boundaries; the extremely-severe class was added in 2015 per that page).
**Links:** DM-311, DM-313, DM-315, DM-316.
**Trap:** Knots and km/h thresholds differ by source; the boundary km/h values above conflict between owner and Wikipedia, so quote the knot values or check IMD's current bulletin. Depressions are unnamed yet cause major floods.
**Mains hook:** Use the classification to argue that under-warned Depression stages drive inland flooding (see DM-340 Chennai 2015). Qualified close: naming the category matters less than the action each category triggers.
**Back:** *"Named from Cyclonic Storm (34 kt); Super is 120 kt and up."*

### Flashcard DM-315 — IMD four colour codes
**Front:** What do IMD's Green, Yellow, Orange and Red warning colours mean?
**Definition (learn):** Colour codes communicate action level for a forecast cyclone or weather alert.
**Breakdown:**
- Green = no warning.
- Yellow = "be updated" (watch and stay informed).
- Orange = "be prepared" (extremely bad weather; possible blackouts and communication disruption).
- Red = "take action" (severe weather expected; significant life risk).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §2, PDF p.37; ⚠️ IMD page not fetched).
**Links:** DM-316 (bulletin lead-time sequence), DM-327 (warning-to-action chain), DM-314.
**Trap:** Colour codes measure action level, not lead time. Red is a forecast-based action signal; it does not guarantee the forecast event will occur.
**Mains hook:** A 2022 GS-I question asked for the meaning of colour-coded warnings for cyclone-prone areas (owner PYQ integration). Answer: explain each colour's action and the household/authority response, then close on the warning-to-action gap, not on the colours alone.
**Back:** *"G-Y-O-R: Green sleeps, Yellow watches, Orange prepares, Red acts."*

### Flashcard DM-316 — IMD four-stage cyclone bulletin lead-time scheme
**Front:** Name the four bulletin stages and their minimum lead times before landfall.
**Definition (learn):** IMD's cyclone bulletin sequence tells authorities how far ahead a threat is expected.
**Breakdown:**
- Pre-Cyclone Watch — at least 72 hours ahead.
- Cyclone Alert — at least 48 hours.
- Cyclone Warning — at least 24 hours.
- Post-Landfall Outlook — at least 12 hours before landfall.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner cites IMD Cyclone Warning SOP, 23 March 2021 — ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** DM-315, DM-314, DM-327.
**Trap:** Do not conflate this lead-time sequence with the Green/Yellow/Orange/Red action codes; both can apply to one system at once.
**Mains hook:** Use the sequence to show that a 12-hour window is not evacuation time for a remote coastline; evacuation must start on the 48–72-hour stage. Conclude with the need to pair bulletins with pre-positioned transport.
**Back:** *"72-48-24-12: Watch, Alert, Warning, Outlook — lead time, not colour."*

### Flashcard DM-317 — ACWC/CWC network and Cyclone Warning Dissemination System
**Front:** Which IMD centres issue cyclone warnings, and what is the dissemination system?
**Definition (learn):** IMD runs Area Cyclone Warning Centres (ACWCs) and Cyclone Warning Centres (CWCs) under a satellite-based Cyclone Warning Dissemination System.
**Breakdown:**
- ACWCs: Kolkata, Chennai, Mumbai.
- CWCs: Bhubaneswar, Visakhapatnam, Ahmedabad.
- Supported by Doppler radar and satellite observations (owner).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §2, PDF p.36; ⚠️ station list not fetched).
**Links:** DM-315, DM-316, DM-318.
**Trap:** Confusing ACWC with CWC is a close-option trap; the ACWCs are the three larger metro-based centres, the CWCs are the three others listed.
**Mains hook:** Name the institution precisely in any warning answer. A qualified point: the detection chain can work while the last-mile action chain fails (owner adv §2).
**Back:** *"Three ACWCs (Kolkata, Chennai, Mumbai); three CWCs (Bhubaneswar, Vizag, Ahmedabad)."*

### Flashcard DM-318 — Aircraft Probing of Cyclone (APC)
**Front:** What is Aircraft Probing of Cyclone and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** A recommended facility using aircraft to observe the upper-air and cloud-aerosol environment of the cyclone core.
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: understand the cyclone-core environment.
- Listed in the owner as a recommendation in the NDMA cyclone framework.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §4, PDF p.36; ⚠️ current operational status not verified).
**Links:** DM-317, DM-319, DM-327.
**Trap:** A guideline recommending a capability is not proof of nationwide deployment; do not assert APC is operational without a current source.
**Mains hook:** Use APC as an example of a recommended technology whose deployment is unverified, supporting the advanced point that detection tools and operational capacity differ.
**Back:** *"Recommended is not running: probe the core, verify the status."*

### Flashcard DM-319 — NDMA National Guidelines on Management of Cyclones (2008)
**Front:** What is the NDMA's cyclone guideline and its core premise?
**Definition (learn):** The 2008 NDMA guidelines set the national framework for cyclone management.
**Breakdown:**
- Premise: mitigation must be multi-sectoral (owner adv §3).
- Covers warning, shelters, evacuation, structural and ecosystem protection, and communication.
- Sectors named in owner advanced analysis: meteorology (IMD), shelters and infrastructure, coastal-zone regulation, communication (NDCI), agriculture and embankments.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §5; ✅ owner 07-adv §3; year 2008 as owner states).
**Links:** DM-320 (NCRMP), DM-324 (CDMIS), DM-312.
**Trap:** "Multi-sectoral" as a label is not an answer; name the sectors and what each delivers.
**Mains hook:** Use the multi-sectoral premise to argue that cyclone resilience cannot be one ministry's job; close by naming the coordination gap at the apex operations centre (owner adv §5).
**Back:** *"2008: many hands, one storm — name the sectors."*

### Flashcard DM-320 — National Cyclone Risk Mitigation Project (NCRMP)
**Front:** What is NCRMP, which States it covers, and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** A structural and non-structural cyclone-mitigation project covering 13 cyclone-prone States/UTs, implemented by NDMA under MHA with NIDM, partly World Bank-funded.
**Breakdown:**
- Category I (higher vulnerability): Andhra Pradesh, Gujarat, Odisha, Tamil Nadu, West Bengal.
- Category II (lower vulnerability): Maharashtra, Karnataka, Kerala, Goa, Puducherry, Lakshadweep, Daman and Diu, Andaman and Nicobar.
- Measures: shelters, evacuation roads, saline embankments, bio-shields, warning dissemination.
- Phase I closed December 2018 (₹2,524.84 crore); Phase II closed March 2023 (₹1,806.84 crore) — 📰 reported by MHA/PIB 1 April 2026 (⚠️ PIB not reachable, 403).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §2, §5; ⚠️ phase costs unverified).
**Links:** DM-319, DM-321, DM-322, DM-324, DM-327.
**Trap:** Describe NCRMP in the past tense as completed. No dated source confirms a Phase III, and MoEFCC's National Coastal Mission is not its successor.
**Mains hook:** Use the Category I/II split to argue for differentiated investment. Advanced close: the live question has shifted from construction to operation and maintenance liability held by States on routine budgets (owner adv §10, a governance inference).
**Back:** *"Thirteen States, two categories, two phases — both closed."*

### Flashcard DM-321 — NCRMP multi-purpose cyclone shelters by State
**Front:** Which States reported the largest counts of multi-purpose cyclone shelters under NCRMP?
**Definition (learn):** State-wise shelter counts reported by MHA in a February 2025 release.
**Breakdown:**
- Odisha 316; Andhra Pradesh 219; West Bengal 146; Gujarat 76; Kerala 17; Goa 11; Karnataka 10 (as reported in owner, citing MHA/PIB 4 Feb 2025).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §5; ⚠️ PIB not verified).
**Links:** DM-320, DM-319.
**Trap:** A shelter built is not a shelter used; counts measure construction, not functioning or accessible shelters.
**Mains hook:** Use the Odisha/Andhra concentration to test whether shelter numbers track vulnerability; qualify with maintenance and community-use evidence.
**Back:** *"Odisha tops shelters — built is not used."*

### Flashcard DM-322 — East versus west coast cyclone asymmetry
**Front:** What share of Bay of Bengal and Arabian Sea storms reach each coast?
**Definition (learn):** Document-period estimate that Bay of Bengal storms are far more frequent on the east coast than Arabian Sea storms on the west.
**Breakdown:**
- Over 58% of Bay of Bengal cyclonic storms approach/cross the east coast in October–November.
- Only 25% of Arabian Sea storms hit the west coast.
- Post-monsoon cyclones are typically more intense (owner).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §3, PDF pp.35-36; ⚠️ document-period; check current IMD climatology).
**Links:** DM-320 (Category I/II), DM-312.
**Trap:** "East and west face equal frequency" is wrong by the owner's estimate; but the figures are document-period and need current IMD climatology.
**Mains hook:** East-coast States justify proportionately higher structural investment. Qualified close: Category II states retain residual risk from rarer events (owner adv §7).
**Back:** *"Fifty-eight east, twenty-five west — Bay storms dominate."*

### Flashcard DM-323 — India's coastline length (2025 remeasurement)
**Front:** What is the official coastline baseline after the 2025 remeasurement?
**Definition (learn):** The GIS/HWL remeasurement gives India's coastline as 11,098.81 km.
**Breakdown:**
- Replaces the older "over 8,000 km" document-period figure (owner).
- Updates exposure and planning baselines but does not itself redraw CRZ high-tide-line boundaries (owner).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner cites PIB 2198800; ⚠️ PIB returned 403, not verified).
**Links:** DM-322, DM-320.
**Trap:** Quoting "8,000 km" as current is a stale-figure trap; quoting 11,098.81 km as proof of CRZ redrawing is another.
**Mains hook:** Use the baseline in exposure arithmetic; qualify that a longer coast is not a measure of vulnerability.
**Back:** *"Eight thousand was old; eleven thousand and change is new."*

### Flashcard DM-324 — Cyclone Disaster Management Information System (CDMIS)
**Front:** What is CDMIS?
**Definition (learn):** A recommended comprehensive online information system for State disaster-management departments covering all DM phases.
**Breakdown:**
- Recommended for State DM departments (owner).
- Covers all phases: prevention to recovery (owner).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §5, PDF p.38; implementation status not verified).
**Links:** DM-319, DM-320, DM-327.
**Trap:** A recommended information system is not an operating one; verify deployment before claiming it.
**Mains hook:** Use CDMIS to argue for integrated data across agencies (owner adv §8, "network integration gap").
**Back:** *"CDMIS: one data spine, all DM phases."*

### Flashcard DM-325 — Cyclone Remal (May 2024)
**Front:** Summarise Cyclone Remal and why it is an applied warning case.
**Definition (learn):** A severe cyclonic storm that made landfall on 26 May 2024 and was used as a worked example of warning, evacuation and response.
**Breakdown:**
- Landfall date 26 May 2024 (✅ Wikipedia: Cyclone_Remal).
- Peak 3-minute sustained wind 110 km/h as reported (Wikipedia gives no landfall-specific wind); gusts 140 km/h (✅ Wikipedia).
- Deaths 61+ per Wikipedia infobox: at least 10 Bangladesh, 34 Mizoram, 3 Assam, 1 Meghalaya, 13 Telangana (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner says landfall "between Sagar Island and Khepupara" with pre-emptive evacuation and NDRF/ICG/Navy readiness (⚠️ landfall location conflicts with Wikipedia, which places landfall near Bangladesh/Mongla).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ Wikipedia: Cyclone_Remal; ⚠️ owner PIB link unverified).
**Links:** DM-317, DM-320, DM-327.
**Trap:** Do not cite the Sagar Island–Khepupara claim as settled; cite the 26 May 2024 date and reported impacts; Remal's lesson is the cascade (north-east flood and landslide), not a wind-speed record.
**Mains hook:** Use Remal to test the warning-to-evacuation chain: evacuation worked where warning reached the last mile, yet deaths arose from inland cascades. Qualified conclusion: warnings reduce coastal deaths but do not cover inland spillover.
**Back:** *"Remal: May 26, cascade beyond the coast."*

### Flashcard DM-326 — 1999 Odisha super cyclone
**Front:** Describe the 1999 Odisha cyclone's date, landfall, toll and industrial damage.
**Definition (learn):** A very severe cyclone that struck Odisha on 29 October 1999 and was one of the deadliest in the North Indian Ocean.
**Breakdown:**
- Landfall 29 October 1999, 06:00 UTC, between Puri and Kendrapara (✅ Wikipedia: 1999_Odisha_cyclone).
- Peak 3-minute sustained wind 260 km/h (IMD) (✅ Wikipedia).
- Official death toll 9,887 (Government/IMD figure); EM-DAT 10,915; some estimates up to 30,000 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Industrial damage: Oswal Chemicals and Fertilisers diammonium-phosphate plant severely damaged; Paradip Port damage put at ₹300 million (✅ Wikipedia).
- Owner claim that a phosphoric-acid sludge containment was damaged is not in the fetched Wikipedia text (⚠️).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ Wikipedia: 1999_Odisha_cyclone).
**Links:** DM-312, DM-325, DM-394 (NaTech), DM-408 (Kandla), DM-319.
**Trap:** Do not state the phosphoric-acid-sludge detail as verified; the verified industrial impact is the DAP plant and port damage. Do not confuse with the 1994 Odisha cyclone, which the owner uses for flood cascade.
**Mains hook:** Use 1999 as the historical benchmark for storm surge and NaTech. Qualified conclusion: mortality fell in later Odisha cyclones because of shelters and warning, while economic exposure rose.
**Back:** *"Nineteen-ninety-nine, Puri to Kendrapara, nine thousand eight-eighty-seven."*

### Flashcard DM-327 — Concept: cyclone warning-to-action chain
**Front:** Why is the cyclone problem a warning-to-action conversion problem, not only detection?
**Definition (learn):** Cyclone risk runs through a chain: cyclogenesis → wind, rain and surge exposure → IMD warning → evacuation and shelter → restoration.
**Breakdown:**
- Technical chain: ACWC/CWC, Doppler radar, colour codes, bulletins (DM-315 to DM-317).
- Human/institutional chain: household response, evacuation, shelter use, local planning.
- Owner names the failure to respond to warnings and lack of grassroots participation as challenges (PDF p.37).
- Apex operations centre not fully automated (owner adv §5).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 07-basic §3, §13; ✅ owner 07-adv §2).
**Links:** DM-315, DM-316, DM-320, DM-325, DM-326, DM-313.
**Trap:** NCRMP's existence does not prove coastal protection; shelters and warnings can exist while households do not act.
**Mains hook:** 15-mark coastal-preparedness answer; 20-mark evaluation of cyclone resilience. Qualified conclusion: reduced mortality alone cannot prove lower economic exposure or durable resilience.
**Back:** *"Forecast is not refuge: track the conversion, not the colour."*

### GROUP — Riverine Floods and Urban Flood Resilience

### Flashcard DM-328 — Flood definition and flood-prone area
**Front:** Define a flood and state India's flood-prone area as reported.
**Definition (learn):** A flood is a state of high water along a river channel or on the coast leading to inundation of land.
**Breakdown:**
- Rashtriya Barh Ayog assessed more than 40 million hectares as flood-prone (owner, document-period).
- Flood types: riverine, coastal, flash, urban.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 08-basic §2, PDF p.26; ⚠️ 40 million ha not verified; document-period).
**Links:** DM-329, DM-330, DM-333.
**Trap:** The 40-million-hectare figure is not a current official area; verify against CWC/NDMA before precise use.
**Mains hook:** Use the definition to separate inundation from damage; flood-prone area is exposure, not loss.
**Back:** *"High water over land is a flood; forty million hectares is an old count."*

### Flashcard DM-329 — Urban flood versus riverine flood: peak and volume multiplication
**Front:** How much do urbanised catchments amplify flood peaks and volumes?
**Definition (learn):** Urbanisation makes floods faster and larger than in equivalent rural catchments.
**Breakdown:**
- Flood peaks increase 1.8 to 8 times (owner).
- Flood volumes increase up to 6 times (owner).
- Flooding occurs very quickly; owner says in a matter of minutes.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 08-basic §1, PDF p.27; ⚠️ factor not verified against an external hydrology source).
**Links:** DM-330, DM-334, DM-335, REVISION-CHART §12.
**Trap:** Urban flood is not "riverine flood happening in a city." The multiplication is a land-use and drainage effect, not a rainfall-only effect.
**Mains hook:** 2024 Q18 causes limb: explain amplified runoff from impervious surfaces and blocked drains. Qualified close: the causal share attributable to climate versus land use needs event-specific attribution.
**Back:** *"Peak times eight, volume times six — concrete, not rain alone."*

### Flashcard DM-330 — Urban flood definition and NDMA delinking
**Front:** How is urban flooding defined, and why did NDMA separate it from riverine flood?
**Definition (learn):** Urban flood is caused by excessive runoff in urban areas from overburdened drainage and unregulated construction.
**Breakdown:**
- Distinct from riverine flood in mechanism, peak, speed and governance.
- Owner's Vision answer says NDMA "de-linked Urban Flooding from the subject of (riverine) Floods for the first time," with Chennai as the trigger example (owner 08-adv §4; PDF pp.71-72).
- Governance: ULBs manage locally; national coordination via a recommended Urban Flooding Cell.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 08-basic §2, §6; NDMA Guidelines on Management of Urban Flooding, 2010 (owner)).
**Links:** DM-329, DM-334, DM-340, DM-344.
**Trap:** Delinking is an institutional categorisation, not proof that urban flood is solved by a separate body.
**Mains hook:** Use delinking as evidence for the governance-failure thesis (owner adv §2). Qualified close: a separate category without drainage investment changes nothing physical.
**Back:** *"Urban flood: drains overrun, not rivers overflow."*

### Flashcard DM-331 — Floodplain zoning
**Front:** What is floodplain zoning and how are zones classified?
**Definition (learn):** Regulating land use in flood plains to restrict damage while keeping benefits.
**Breakdown:**
- Vulnerable areas mapped as extremely affected (red) or partially affected (blue) zones (owner).
- A non-structural measure (REVISION-CHART §4).
- Advanced owner: encroachment for habitation and development is a main cause in the Ganga region (PDF p.27).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 08-basic §2, §4, PDF pp.28; ⚠️ zone colours as owner states).
**Links:** DM-332, DM-333, DM-334.
**Trap:** Zoning is non-structural: it changes land use, not embankment height. Zoning enforcement conflicts with housing demand (owner adv §7).
**Mains hook:** 15-mark route: zoning as the main non-structural tool, with the enforcement-versus-housing trade-off. Qualified conclusion: stricter zoning without rehousing options is politically costly.
**Back:** *"Red zones are extreme, blue partial — and enforcement is the hard part."*

### Flashcard DM-332 — Flood proofing
**Front:** Define flood proofing and give examples.
**Definition (learn):** A combination of structural change and emergency action that avoids evacuation: adapting buildings to flooding.
**Breakdown:**
- Examples: raised platforms, elevated drinking-water infrastructure, double-storey houses with first-floor shelter use (owner, PDF pp.28-29).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 08-basic §2).
**Links:** DM-331, DM-333, DM-329.
**Trap:** Flood proofing is not evacuation; it assumes the household can shelter in place.
**Mains hook:** Equity: flood-proofing assumes capital that poor households lack (owner adv §6). Qualified conclusion: subsidised flood-proofing needed for low-income housing.
**Back:** *"Stay up, don't run: platform, first floor, elevated tap."*

### Flashcard DM-333 — Structural versus non-structural flood measures and the Damodar limit
**Front:** Name structural and non-structural flood measures and the documented limit of dams.
**Definition (learn):** Structural measures are physical works; non-structural measures are rules, plans and incentives.
**Breakdown:**
- Structural: reservoirs/dams, embankments/levees, drainage improvement, channel desilting/dredging, diversion (Srinagar spill channel; Delhi supplementary drain), catchment treatment.
- Non-structural: floodplain zoning, flood proofing, Flood Management Plans, Integrated Water Resources Management, flood forecasting.
- Limit: owner records that Damodar-basin dams "could not control the flood" (PDF p.28).
- Yamuna-near-Delhi embankments cited by owner as successful.
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 08-basic §4; ✅ owner 08-adv §3).
**Links:** DM-331, DM-332, DM-330, REVISION-CHART §12 (embankments can transfer risk).
**Trap:** "More embankments always reduce floods" is wrong: embankments can fail and encourage exposure behind them.
**Mains hook:** Use the Damodar case to argue against over-reliance on structural measures. Conclude: a hybrid with zoning and forecasting, not dams alone.
**Back:** *"Dams hold, embankments fail: zone, forecast, and don't trust one wall."*

### Flashcard DM-334 — Urban Flooding Cell (UFC)
**Front:** What is the Urban Flooding Cell and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** A cell recommended in NDMA's 2010 Urban Flooding guidelines, to be constituted in the then-Ministry of Urban Development to coordinate national urban-flood activities.
**Breakdown:**
- National coordination by UFC; ULBs responsible locally.
- Owner's existing-challenges list: unsatisfactory coordination, lack of information sharing, disintegrated investment (PDF p.31).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 08-basic §2, PDF p.31; ⚠️ current constitution not verified; check MoHUA notification).
**Links:** DM-330, DM-335, DM-337.
**Trap:** A cell "recommended" by a guideline is not a constituted body. Owner explicitly flags verification.
**Mains hook:** "Creating a cell therefore manages floods" is the fallacy (owner adv §8). Conclude with coordination mechanisms and budgets, not an office.
**Back:** *"Recommended cell, not a proven cell — verify the notification."*

### Flashcard DM-335 — Flood forecasting: CWC discharge, IMD rainfall and the April 2026 SOP
**Front:** Which agencies supply flood-forecast inputs, and what does the CWC SOP list?
**Definition (learn):** Flood forecasting combines river discharge data (CWC) and rainfall data (IMD).
**Breakdown:**
- CWC field formations collect discharge (hydro-met) data.
- IMD supplies daily rainfall data.
- 📰 CWC April 2026 SOP: 350 forecasting stations — 200 level-forecast and 150 inflow-forecast (owner; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Urban Doppler radar gives 3–6 hours' lead time; nowcasting gives 5–30 minutes (owner, PDF p.30).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (✅ owner 08-basic §5; 📰 CWC SOP April 2026 ⚠️ unverified).
**Links:** DM-336, DM-334, DM-344, DM-329.
**Trap:** Forecast lead time is not the time the public has; official processing consumes some (owner adv §5). Do not cite the station count as current without the dated SOP.
**Mains hook:** Use the nowcasting window to argue that an alert with minute-scale lead time needs automatic public channels. Qualified close.
**Back:** *"CWC gives water, IMD gives rain — forecast needs both."*

### Flashcard DM-336 — CWC river-stage thresholds
**Front:** Name CWC's three river-stage thresholds and the operational colour mapping.
**Definition (learn):** Site-specific gauge levels used in flood forecasting.
**Breakdown:**
- Warning Level — normally about 1 metre below Danger Level, but fixed site by site.
- Danger Level — site-specific.
- Highest Flood Level (HFL) — highest previously recorded level at the site.
- Owner's mapping: Warning-to-Danger = Above Normal/Yellow; Danger-to-HFL = Severe/Orange; above HFL = Extreme/Red (CWC SOP, April 2026).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 CWC SOP April 2026 per owner; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Links:** DM-335, DM-315.
**Trap:** These are site-specific gauge thresholds, not a national numerical standard. Do not quote a single national danger level.
**Mains hook:** Use the site specificity to argue for local calibration of warnings. Qualified close.
**Back:** *"W-D-H: Warning is about one metre below Danger; HFL is the scar."*

### Flashcard DM-337 — Urban Flood Risk Management Programme (UFRMP)
**Front:** What is UFRMP, which cities are covered, and what are the approvals?
**Definition (learn):** An NDMF-financed mitigation programme for urban floods.
**Breakdown:**
- Chennai approved 7 December 2023 (₹561.29 crore, including ₹500 crore central assistance).
- Six cities approved 25 July 2024 (aggregate ₹2,514.36 crore): Mumbai, Kolkata, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Ahmedabad, Pune.
- Phase 2 approved 1 October 2025 for 11 cities at ₹2,444.42 crore on 90:10 Centre–State basis.
- Phase-2 city list: see MHA's February 2026 parliamentary reply (owner).
**Acts / Committees / Institutions / Dates:** (📰 owner; ⚠️ approvals not verified against MHA sources).
**Links:** DM-334, DM-330, DM-340, DM-344.
**Trap:** Approval is not completion. Sanctioned works do not prove reduced loss (REVISION-CHART §29 gate).
**Mains hook:** 2024 Q18 "policies and frameworks" limb. Owner says this is the ex-ante mitigation-finance shift, not post-event relief. Qualified close: finance without maintenance does not sustain drainage.
**Back:** *"Mitigation fund, not relief fund — approved is not built."*

### Flashcard DM-338 — Aapda Mitra scheme
**Front:** What is Aapda Mitra and where does it operate?
**Definition (learn):** An NDMA-approved scheme that trains community volunteers for floods, flash floods and urban floods.
**Breakdown:**
- Operates in 30 most flood-prone districts of 25 States (owner, PDF p.29).
- Volunteers trained for response.
